ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϥⲕⲓ̈ⲱⲟⲩ· ⲛⲟⲩⲧⲃⲛⲟⲟⲩⲉ ⲛⲁⲙⲟⲟⲛⲉ ⲙⲡⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲛⲟⲩⲙⲁ ⲉϥⲕⲓ̈ⲱⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϥⲟⲩⲟϣⲥ̅· ⲛⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲙⲁⲥⲉ· ⲙⲛ ⲛⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲉϩⲉ ⲉⲧⲉⲣ ϩⲱⲃ ⲉⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲉⲙ ⲧⲱϩ ⲉϥⲧⲉϩ ϩⲓ̈ ⲓ̈ⲱⲧ ⲉϥⲧⲃⲃⲏⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁϣϣⲡⲉ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲉⲧϫⲟⲥⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲥⲓⲃⲓ̈ⲧ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲉⲧϩⲗⲟⲩⲗⲱⲟⲩ· ⲛϭⲓ ⲟⲩⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲉϥⲥⲱⲕ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ· ϩⲟⲧⲁⲛ ⲉϣⲁⲛϩⲁϩ ⲧⲁⲕⲟ· ⲛⲧⲉⲡⲡⲩⲣⲅⲟⲥ ϩⲉ· ⲛⲧⲉⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲙⲡⲟⲟϩ ⲣ̅ ⲑⲉ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲙⲡⲣⲏ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓⲛ ⲙⲡⲣⲏ ⲛⲥⲁϣϥ̅ ⲛⲕⲱⲃ ⲙⲡⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ· ⲉϥϣⲁⲛⲧⲁⲗϭⲉ ⲡⲟⲩⲱϣϥ̅ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥⲗⲁⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϥϣⲁⲛⲧⲁⲗϭⲟ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲕⲁⲥ ⲙⲡⲉϥⲥⲁϣ· ⲉⲓⲥ ⲡⲣⲁⲛ ⲙⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛⲏⲩ ϩⲓ̈ⲧⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓϣ· ⲟⲩϭⲱⲛⲧ ⲉϥⲙⲟⲩϩ ⲙⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲉⲟⲟⲩ ⲡⲉ ⲡϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛ̅ⲛⲉϥⲥⲡⲟ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲙ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲕⲱϩⲧ· “ ⲡⲉϥⲡⲛⲁ ⲛⲏⲩ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲉϥⲥⲱⲕ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲉⲓⲁ ϣⲁ ⲡⲕⲁϩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲡⲱϣ ⲉϣⲧⲟⲣⲧⲣ ⲛ̅ⲛϩⲉⲑⲛⲟⲥ ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲧⲉⲩⲡⲗⲁⲛⲏ ⲉⲧϣⲟⲩⲉⲓ̈ⲧ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲟⲩⲛ ⲟⲩⲡⲗⲁⲛⲏ ⲛⲁⲡⲱⲧ ⲛ̅ⲥⲱⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲛⲁϫⲓ̈̅ⲧⲟⲩ ⲙⲡⲉⲩⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ· “ ⲙⲏ ϩⲟⲡⲥ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲉⲩⲫⲣⲁⲛⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓϣ ⲛⲓ̈ⲙ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓϣ ⲛⲓ̈ⲙ ⲉⲛⲁⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲁⲁⲃ ⲛ̅ⲑⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲣ̅ ϣⲁ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲧⲉⲩⲫⲣⲁⲛⲉ ⲉⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲙⲛ ⲟⲩⲥⲏϥⲉ ⲛ̅ϫⲱ ⲉⲡⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲙⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ· ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ̅ ⲙⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲉⲧϫⲟⲟⲣ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲁⲁⲃ ⲙⲡⲓ̅ⲏ̅ⲗ̅· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲧⲣⲉⲩⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲉⲡⲉⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲉϥⲥⲙⲏ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲡϭⲱⲛⲧ ⲙⲡⲉϥϭⲃⲟⲓ̈· ⲛϥ̅ⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲏⲧⲉ ⲉϫⲱⲟⲩ ⲛϭⲟⲛ̅ⲥ̅· ⲙⲛ ⲟⲩϭⲱⲛⲧ ⲛⲟⲣⲅⲏ· ⲙⲛ ⲟⲩϣⲁϩ ⲛⲟⲣⲅⲏ· ⲉⲥⲟⲩⲱⲙ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲭⲁⲗⲁ{ⲥ}ⲍⲁ ⲉⲩⲛⲏⲩ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ ϩⲓ̈ ⲟⲩⲥⲟⲡ ⲛϭⲟⲛⲥ· ϩⲓⲧⲙ ⲡⲉϩⲣⲟⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙ̅ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ· ⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓⲟⲥ ⲛⲁϭⲱⲧⲡ· ϩⲛ̅ ⲧⲉⲡⲗⲩⲅⲏ ⲉⲧϥ̅ⲛⲁⲣⲁϩⲧⲟⲩ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲁϥ ⲙⲡⲉϥⲕⲱⲧⲉ:· ⲙⲡⲙⲁ
rich. (And) on that day your cattle will graze in a fertile and spacious place; 24 your bulls and your oxen that work the land will eat chaff mixed with winnowed barley. 25 And on that day on every lofty mountain and every high hill there will be running water, on that day when many perish (and when) the tower falls 26 and the light of the moon will be like the light of the sun, and the light of the sun (will be) sevenfold on that day when he heals the destruction of his people, and when he heals the pain of your wound. 27 See, the name of the Lord comes after a long time; a burning wrath with glory is the word of his lips; the word is full of anger, and the anger of his wrath will devour like fire; 28 his breath, like water sweeping in a ravine, comes to the earth and will be divided to confuse the nations in their vain error, and error will pursue them and lay hold of them face-to-face. 29 Must you continually rejoice and continually enter my holy (places) as those who make (a) feast and rejoice? (Must you) come in with a flute to the mountain of God, to the strong God and the Holy of Israel? 30 And God will make the glory of his voice to be heard, and he will show the wrath of his arm and he will thunder over them violently and with angry wrath and a devouring flame of anger; and like water and hail falling down together with violence. 31 The Assyrians will be defeated because of the voice of the Lord, through the stroke with which he will smite them. 32 And this will happen to him on his


[p. 618] {#po.57.p618}
ⲉⲧⲉⲣⲉⲑⲉⲗⲡⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛⲧⲉϥⲃⲟⲏⲑⲓ̈ⲁ ⲙⲙⲟϥ· ⲧⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲛⲉϥⲕⲱ ⲛϩⲧⲏϥ ⲉⲣⲟⲥ· ⲛⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲛⲉⲩⲛⲁⲙⲓ̅ϣⲉ ⲛⲙⲙⲁϥ ϩⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲩϭⲓⲑⲁⲣⲁ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉⲩⲕⲟⲩⲕⲙ̅ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲩⲙⲛⲧⲁⲃⲟⲗⲏ··'. ⲥⲉⲛⲁϣⲁⲁⲧⲕ ⲅⲁⲣ ϩⲁⲑⲏ ⲛⲛⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ· ⲙⲏ ⲁⲩⲥⲟⲃⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲕ ϩⲱⲱⲕ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲕⲣ̅ ⲣⲣⲟ· ⲟⲩⲓ̈ⲁ ⲉϥϣⲁⲕϩ̅· ϩⲉⲛϣⲏ ⲛ̅ⲕⲉⲇⲣⲟⲥ· ⲟⲩⲕⲱϩⲧ ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ϩⲛϣⲉ ⲉⲛⲁϣⲱⲟⲩ· ⲉⲣⲉⲡϭⲱⲛⲧ ⲙ̅ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ· ⲟ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲓ̈ⲁ ⲉϥⲙⲟⲩϩ ϩⲓ̈ⲧⲛ̅
side, in the place where is the hope of his help in which he (himself) trusted; they in their turns will wage war against him with their lyres and their drums, 33 for you will be deceived before the days; was it also made ready for you to reign? A deep trench, cedar woods, fire and wood in abundance. The wrath of the Lord is like a ravine burning with brimstone.
ⲟⲩⲑⲏⲛ· ' ⲟⲩⲟⲓ̈ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲃⲏⲕ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ ⲉ̅ⲕⲏⲙⲉ ⲛⲥⲁ ⲃⲟⲏⲑⲓ̈ⲁ· ⲛⲉⲧⲕⲱ ⲛϩⲧⲏⲩ ⲉϩⲛϩⲧⲱⲱⲣ ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ϩⲉⲛϩⲁⲣⲙⲁ· ⲛⲁϣⲱⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲛⲉϩⲧⲱⲱⲣ ⲉⲧⲟϣ ⲉⲙⲁⲧⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙ̅ⲡⲟⲩⲛⲁϩⲧⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲁⲁⲃ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓ̅ⲏ̅ⲗ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙ̅ⲡⲟⲩϣⲓ̈ⲛⲉ ⲛ̅ⲥⲁ ⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ⲧⲟϥ ⲟⲩⲥⲟⲫⲟⲥ ⲡⲉ· ⲁϥⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲉϫⲱⲟⲩ ⲛϩⲉⲛⲡⲉⲑⲟⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥⲛⲉⲩⲁⲑⲁⲓ̈ⲧⲉⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥϣⲁϫⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲛⲏⲓ̈ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲙ̅ⲡⲟⲛⲏⲣⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲧⲉⲩϩⲉⲗⲡⲓ̈ⲥ ⲉⲧϣⲟⲩⲉⲓⲧ· ⲟⲩⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲛ̅ⲣⲙ̅ⲛ̅ⲕⲏⲙⲉ· ⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲁⲛ· ϩⲉⲛⲥⲁⲣⲝ̅ ⲛϩⲧⲟ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϩⲉⲛⲃⲟⲏϭⲓ̈ⲁ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲉ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓⲥ ⲇⲉ ⲛⲁⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲧⲉϥϭⲓϫ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲉϫⲱⲟⲩ· “ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϩⲓⲥⲉ ⲛϭⲓ̈ ⲛⲉⲧⲃⲟⲏⲑⲉⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲧⲁⲕⲟ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ϩⲓ̈ ⲟⲩⲥⲟⲡ· “ ϫⲉ ⲧⲁⲓ ⲧⲉ ⲑⲉ ⲛⲧⲁⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲓ̈· ϫⲉ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲉϣⲁⲣⲉⲡⲙⲟⲩⲓ̅ ⲱϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲏ ⲡⲙⲁⲥ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲩⲓ̅ ⲉϫⲉⲛ ⲧⲡⲁϩⲥ̅ ⲉⲧϥ̅ⲛⲁⲧⲁϩⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ϥⲱϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉϫⲱⲥ· ϣⲁⲛⲧⲉⲛ̅ⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲙⲟⲩϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ̅ ⲧⲉⲥⲙⲏ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥϭⲱⲛⲧ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ⲥⲉϣⲧⲟⲣⲧⲣ̅ ⲉⲧⲃⲉ ⲡⲁϣⲁⲓ̈ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥϭⲱⲛⲧ· ⲧⲁⲓ ⲧⲉ ⲑⲉ ⲉⲧⲉⲣⲉⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲥⲁⲃⲁⲱⲑ ⲛⲏⲩ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ ⲉⲥⲱⲣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉϫⲙ̅ ⲡⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲥⲓ̈ⲱⲛ· ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲥⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲛϩⲛ̅ϩⲁⲗⲏⲧ ⲉⲩϩⲏⲗ ⲧⲁⲓ̈ ⲧⲉ ⲑⲉ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛⲁⲣ̅
31 1 Woe to those who go down to Egypt for help, those who trust in horses and in chariots, for they are many, and in horses — a great multitude — but did not trust in the Holy One of Israel and did not seek God! 2 And he is wise, (and) he brought evils upon them; his word will not be set to naught. And he will rise against the houses of the evil men and against their vain hope — 3 an Egyptian man, not God, flesh of horses, and they are not helps. But the Lord will turn his hand against them, and those who help them will grow weary, and they will all perish together. 4 Because thus the Lord said to me: As the lion or the whelp of lion might roar over the prey that he will take and he shall cry out over it until the mountains are filled with the voice of his wrath — and they shall be terrified, at the greatness of his wrath — so the Lord Sabaoth will come down, to march against Mount Sion (and) against its mountains. 5 Like birds flying, so the Lord will shield lerousalem; and he


[p. 620] {#po.57.p620}
ⲛⲁϣⲧⲉ ⲉⲓ̅ⲉⲣⲟⲩⲥⲁⲗⲏⲙ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲛⲟⲩϩⲙ̅ ⲛ̅ϥ̅ⲧⲟⲩϫⲟ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ϥ̅ⲧⲁⲛϩⲟ· ⲕⲉⲧ ⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲧⲙⲉⲉⲩⲉ ⲉⲡϣⲟϫⲛⲉ ⲉⲑⲏⲡ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲁⲛⲟⲙⲟⲛ· ” ϫⲉ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̅ⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲛⲁϥⲓ̈ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲛⲉⲩⲙⲟⲩⲛ̅ⲅ̅ ⲛϭⲓϫ ⲛϩⲁⲧ ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲛ̅ⲛⲟⲩⲃ· ⲛⲉⲛⲧⲁⲛⲉⲩⲧⲏⲏⲃⲉ ⲧⲁⲙⲓⲟⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲥⲥⲟⲩⲣ ⲛⲁϩⲉ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲥⲏⲃⲉ ⲛ̅ⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲁⲛ· ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲥⲏⲃⲉ ⲛ̅ⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲁⲟⲩⲟⲙϥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲡⲱⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲛ ϩⲏⲧⲥ ⲛⲟⲩⲥⲏⲃⲉ ⲛ̅ϩⲣ̅ϣⲓ̈ⲣⲉ· ⲥⲉⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲩϭⲟⲧⲡ· ⲥⲉⲛⲁϣⲟϫⲡⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲡⲉⲧⲣⲁ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩϣⲗ̅ϩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ⲥⲉϭⲱⲧⲡ· ⲡⲉⲧⲡⲱⲧ ⲇⲉ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϭⲟⲡϥ̅· ⲛⲁⲓ̈ ⲛⲉⲧⲉⲣⲉⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲟⲩ·
will deliver and preserve and save (it). 6 Turn, you who plan the deep and lawless plan, 7 because on that day people shall take away their handiworks of silver and gold, which their fingers have made. 8 And Assour shall fall not with a man’s dagger; nor a human dagger shall devour him, and he shall not flee from before a dagger, (his) young men shall be defeated; 9 for they shall be left in a rock, as (in) a rampart, and they shall be defeated, and the one who flees will be caught. This is what the Lord says: “Happy is the one who has a seed in Sion and kinsmen in lerousalem.”
ϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲓ̈ⲁⲧϥ̅ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲧⲉ ⲟⲩⲛ̅ⲧϥ ⲟⲩⲥⲡⲉⲣⲙⲁ ϩⲛ̅ ⲥⲓ̈ⲱⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϩⲛ̅ⲣⲙ̅ⲛⲏⲓ̈ ϩⲛ̅ ⲓ̅ⲉⲣⲟⲩⲥⲁⲗⲏⲙ· ' ⲉⲓⲥ ⲟⲩⲣⲣⲟ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛ̅ⲇⲓ̈ⲕⲁⲓ̈ⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲣ̅ⲣⲟ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲁⲣⲭⲱⲛ ⲛⲁⲁⲣⲭⲉⲓ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩϩⲁⲡ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲡⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲉⲧⲛⲁϩⲱⲡ ⲛⲛⲉϥϣⲁϫⲉ· ϥⲛⲁϩⲱⲡ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲥⲱⲕ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟϥ ϩⲓ̈ⲧⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲙⲟⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛ̅ϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ̅ ⲥⲓ̈ⲱⲛ ⲛ̅ⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲉⲓⲉⲣⲟ ⲉϥⲥⲱⲕ ⲉϥϩⲁ ⲉⲟⲟⲩ· ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲕⲁϩ ⲉϥⲟⲃⲉ· ' ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲩⲛⲁϩⲧⲉ ⲉⲣⲱⲙⲉ· ⲙ̅ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϯ ⲛ̅ⲛⲉⲩⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲉⲥⲱⲧⲙ̅· “ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲛ̅ϭⲱⲃ ⲛⲁϯ ϩⲧⲏⲩ ⲉϫⲓ ⲥⲙⲏ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲗⲁⲥ ⲉⲧϭⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲥⲃⲱ ⲉϣⲁϫⲉ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲉⲓⲣⲏⲛⲏ ϩⲛ ⲟⲩϭⲉⲡⲏ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲛⲉⲩⲕⲟⲧⲟⲩ ⲉϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲙⲡⲥⲟϭ ϫⲉ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲁⲣⲭⲱⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲛⲉⲟⲩϩⲩⲡⲉⲣⲉⲧⲏⲥ ⲍϫⲟⲟⲥⲝ ϫⲓⲛ ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ϫⲉ ⲕⲁ ⲣⲱ· ϭ ⲡⲥⲟϭ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲁϫⲱ ⲛϩⲛⲙⲛⲧⲥⲟϭ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϥϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲁⲛⲟⲓ̈ ⲛϩⲛⲡⲉⲧϣⲟⲩⲉⲓⲧ· ⲉϫⲱⲕ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲛⲁⲛⲟⲙⲟⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϣⲁϫⲉ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ ⲉⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛⲟⲩⲡⲗⲁⲛⲏ· ⲉϫⲱⲱⲣⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛϩⲛ̅ⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲉⲩϩⲕⲟⲉⲓⲧ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲡⲱϩⲧ ⲛⲛⲉⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲉⲧⲟⲃⲉ· ⲉⲣⲉⲡϣⲁϫⲛⲉ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛ}ⲛ̅ⲙⲡⲟⲛⲏⲣⲟⲥ ⲙⲉⲉⲩⲉ ⲉⲛⲁⲛⲟⲙⲟⲛ· ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲟ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲑⲃⲃⲍⲓ̈ϩⲏⲩ ϩⲛ̅ ϩⲉⲛϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛϫⲓ̈ ⲛϭⲟⲛⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϫⲱⲱⲣⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲛϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲑⲃⲃⲓ̈ⲏⲩ ϩⲛ ⲟⲩϩⲁⲡ· ⲁⲙⲙⲁⲓ̈ⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲇⲉ ⲙⲉⲉⲩⲉ ⲉϩⲛ̅ⲙⲛⲧⲥⲁⲃⲏ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲁⲓ̈ ⲡⲉ ⲡϣⲁϫⲛⲉ ⲉⲧⲛⲁϭⲱ· ⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲉϩⲓ̈ⲟⲙⲉ ⲛⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲥⲱⲧⲙ̅ ⲉⲧⲁⲥⲙⲏ· ⲛϣⲉⲉⲣⲉ ⲉⲧϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩϩⲉⲗⲡⲓ̈ⲥ ⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲉⲛⲁϣⲁϫⲉ· ⲁⲣⲓ ⲡⲙⲉⲉⲩⲉ ⲛⲛⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲣⲟⲙⲡⲉ ϩⲛ
32 1 For see, a just king will reign, and the rulers will rule with judgment. 2 And it will be: the man who will be hiding his words will be hidden as one who is drawn from water, and he will appear in Sion like a rushing river, glorious in a thirsty land. 3 Then no longer will they trust in men, but they will lend their ears to hear. 4 And the heart of the weak will apply itself to hear, and the stammering tongues will soon / learn to speak with peace. 5 And they will no longer tell the fool: “Rule!” And servant shall no longer (say): “Be quiet.” 6 For the fool will speak folly, and his heart will devise vain things in order to accomplish lawless things and to speak error against the Lord, in order to scatter hungry souls and to make empty the souls that thirst. 7 For the counsel of the evil ones will plan lawless things, to ruin the humble ones with unjust words and to scatter the words of the humble ones in judgment. 8 But the godly have planned intelligent things, and this is the counsel that will remain. 9 Rise up, you wealthy women, and hear my voice; you daughters in hope, hear my words. 10 Mention the days of a year in pain


[p. 622] {#po.57.p622}
ⲟⲩⲙⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲙⲛ ⲟⲩϩⲉⲗⲡⲓ̈ⲥ· ⲁⲡϫⲱⲱⲗⲉ ⲟⲩⲱ· ⲁϥⲟⲩⲉⲓⲛⲉ ⲛϭⲓ ⲡϫⲟ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ϥ̅ⲛⲁⲥⲟⲧϥ̅ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲉⲓ̅· ⲡⲱϣⲥ· ⲗⲩⲡⲏ ⲛⲉⲧⲕⲱ ⲛϩⲧⲏⲩ· ⲕⲁ ⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̅ ⲕⲁϩⲏⲩ· ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲕⲏ ⲕⲁϩⲏⲩ· ⲙⲉⲣ ⲛⲉⲧⲛ̅ϯⲡⲉ ⲛϩⲛ̅ϭⲟⲟⲩⲛⲉ· ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲛⲉϩⲡⲓ̈ ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲉⲕⲓ̈ⲃⲉ· ϫⲓ̈ⲛ ⲧⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲙ̅ ⲡⲟⲩⲱϣ ϥ̅). ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲅⲉⲛⲏⲙⲁ ⲛⲧⲃⲱ ⲛⲉⲗⲟⲟⲗⲉ· ⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲙⲡⲁⲗⲁⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲧⲁⲩⲟ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲛϩⲛ̅ϣⲟⲛⲧⲉ ϩⲓ̈ ⲭⲟⲣⲧⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϥⲓ̈ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲛⲟϥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ̅ ⲓ̅ⲗ̅ⲏ̅ⲙ̅ ⲧⲡⲟⲗⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛ̅ⲣⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲟ· ⲛⲏⲓ̈ ⲉⲩⲕⲏ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ· ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲕⲱ ⲛ̅ⲧⲙⲛ̅ⲧⲣ̅ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲟ ⲛ̅ ⲛ̅ⲡⲟⲗⲓ̈ⲥ· ⲛⲏⲓ̈ ⲉⲧⲛⲁⲛⲟⲩⲟⲩ· ⲛⲧⲉⲛϯⲙⲉ ⲣ̅ ⲉⲙ̅ϩⲁⲁⲩ ϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ ⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟϥ ⲛⲛⲓ̈ⲁ ⲛ̅ⲧⲟⲟⲩ· ⲙ̅ⲙⲁ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲛⲉ ⲛⲛⲟϩⲉ· ϣⲁⲛⲧⲉⲟⲩⲡ̅ⲛ̅ⲁ ⲉⲓ ⲉϫⲱⲧⲛ̅ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡϫⲓ̈ⲥⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲭⲉⲣⲙⲉⲗ ⲛⲁⲣ ϫⲁⲓ̈ⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲕⲁⲣⲙⲏⲗⲟⲥ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲟⲡϥ̅ ⲉⲩⲙⲁ ⲛϣⲏⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡϩⲁⲡ ⲛⲁⲙ̅ⲧⲟⲛ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ϩⲛ̅ ⲧⲉⲣⲏⲙⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲇⲓⲕⲁⲓⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱϩ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡⲕⲁⲣⲙⲏⲗⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉϩⲃⲏⲩⲉ ⲛⲧⲇⲓ̈ⲕⲁⲓⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲉⲓⲣⲏⲛⲏ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲇⲓ̈ⲕⲁⲓⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ⲛⲁⲁⲙⲁϩⲧⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲙ̅ⲧⲟⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲁϩⲧⲉ ϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϥⲗⲁⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱϩ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩϩⲉⲗⲡⲓ̈ⲥ ϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϥⲗⲁⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱϩ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲡⲟⲗⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛⲓ̈ⲣⲏⲛⲏ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱϩ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲧⲁϫⲣⲟ· ⲛⲥⲉⲙ̅ⲧⲟⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲟⲩ ϩⲛ̅ⲟⲩⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙⲙⲁⲟ· ⲉⲣϣⲁⲛⲧⲉⲭⲁⲗⲁⲍⲁ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲓ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ ⲛⲛⲉⲥⲉⲓ ⲉϫⲱⲧⲛ̅· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛϭⲓ ⲛⲉⲧⲟⲩⲏϩ ϩⲛ̅ ⲛⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲉⲩⲧⲁϫⲣⲏⲩ
with hope; the vintage has been consumed; the sowing has ceased and will no longer come. 11 Be amazed; be grieved, you who trust; strip and make yourselves bare; gird sackcloth on your loins, 12 and beat your breasts for a desired field and (for) the fruitful vine. 13 The land of my people will produce thorns and grass, and joy will be removed from lerousalem, the wealthy city, 14 forsaken houses! They will give up the wealth of the cities — the good houses. And the villages will be caverns forever, the pleasures of wild donkeys, the feeding places of the flocks, 15 until a spirit from on high comes upon you. And Chermel will be a wilderness, and Carmel will be regarded as a forest. 16 Then the judgment will rest in the wilderness, and the righteousness abide in Carmel. 17 And the works of righteousness will be with peace, and righteousness will hold on to rest, and they (will be) confident forever. 18 And his people will dwell in hope forever; and his people will abide in a city of peace, and will dwell in strength and they will rest with wealth. 19 If hail descends, it will not come upon you. And those who dwell in the fields will be strong, like those who dwell in the plain. 20 Happy are those who sow beside every water, where the ox treads with the donkey.

33 1 Woe to those who distress you! But no one will make you distressed, and the one who rejects does not reject you; for the ones who reject will be destructed and delivered up, and like a moth on a garment, so will they be defeated. 2 O Lord, have mercy on us, for in you we have trusted. May the

ⲉⲣⲟⲕ· ⲙⲁⲣⲉⲡⲉⲥⲡⲉⲣⲙⲁ ⲛⲛⲉⲧⲟ ⲛⲁⲧⲛⲁϩⲧⲉ ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲩⲧⲁⲕⲟ· ⲉⲣⲉⲡⲉⲛⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲇⲉ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡⲉⲩⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓϣ ⲛⲧⲉⲑⲗⲓⲯⲓ̈ⲥ· ⲉⲧⲃⲉ ⲧⲉⲥⲙⲏ ⲛⲧⲉⲕϩⲟⲧⲉ· ⲁⲛⲗⲁⲟⲥ ⲡⲱϣⲥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲧⲉⲕϩⲟⲧⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲛϩⲉⲑⲛⲟⲥ ϫⲱⲱⲣⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ⲇⲉ ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲁⲛⲁϣⲱⲗ ⲛⲁⲥⲱⲟⲩϩ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ· ⲛⲁⲡⲕⲟⲩⲓ̈· ⲙⲛ ⲛⲁⲡⲛⲟϭ· ⲛ̅ⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲁ ⲉϥⲥⲱⲟⲩϩ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲛϩⲛ̅ϣϫⲉ· ⲧⲁⲓ̈ ⲧⲉ ⲑⲉ ⲉⲧⲟⲩⲛⲁⲥⲱⲃⲉ ⲙ̅ⲙⲱⲧⲛ̅· ⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲟⲩⲁⲁⲃ ⲉⲧⲟⲩⲏϩ ϩⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲧϫⲟⲥⲉ· ⲉⲁⲥⲓⲱⲛ ⲙⲟⲩϩ ⲛϩⲁⲡ ϩⲓ̈ ⲇⲓ̅ⲕⲁⲓⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ· ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲧⲁⲁⲩ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡⲛⲟⲙⲟⲥ· ⲉⲣⲉⲡⲉⲛⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ̈ ϩⲛ̅ ⲛⲁϩⲱⲱⲣ· ⲉⲣⲉⲧⲥⲟⲫⲓ̈ⲁ ⲙⲛ ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙⲛϩⲏⲧ· ⲙⲛ̅ ⲧⲙⲛⲧⲣⲙ̅ⲛ̅ⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲏⲩ ⲛϩⲁⲣⲙ̅ⲓ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ· ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲉ ⲛⲁϩⲱⲱⲣ ⲛⲧⲇⲓ̈ⲕⲁⲓ̈ⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ· ⲉⲓⲥ ϩⲏⲏⲧⲉ ⲛⲧⲟⲟⲩ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲣ ϩⲟⲧⲉ ϩⲛ̅ ⲧⲉⲧⲛϩⲟⲧⲉ· ⲛⲉⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲣ̅ ϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲛϩⲏⲧⲟⲩ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲱϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲩⲧⲱⲃϩ̅ ϩⲛ̅ⲟⲩⲉⲓⲣⲏⲛⲏ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓ̈ⲧⲛ̅ ⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̅· ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲧⲛ̅ⲛⲟⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛϩⲛ̅ⲃⲁⲓ̈ϣⲓ̈ⲛⲉ ⲉⲩⲣⲓ̈ⲙⲉ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲥⲓ̈ϣⲉ· ⲉⲩⲧⲱⲃϩ ⲛⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲛⲉϩⲓ̈ⲟⲟⲩⲉ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛ̅ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲁⲣ̅ ϫⲁⲓ̈ⲉ· ⲁⲑⲟⲧⲉ ⲛ̅ⲛϩⲉⲑⲛⲟⲥ ⲗⲟ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϥⲓ ⲛⲧⲇⲓ̈ⲕⲁⲓ̈ⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲛⲁⲓ̈· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ⲛⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲟⲡⲟⲩ ⲉⲣⲱⲙⲉ· ” ⲁⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲣ̅ ϩⲏⲃⲉ· ⲁⲡⲗⲓ̈ⲃⲁⲛⲟⲥ ϫⲓ̈ ϣⲓ̈ⲡⲉ· ⲁⲁⲥⲧⲁⲣⲱⲛ ⲣ̅ ϩⲉⲗⲟⲥ· ⲧⲅⲁⲗⲓ̈ⲗⲁⲓ̈ⲁ· ⲙⲛ ⲡⲕⲁⲣⲙⲏⲗⲟⲥ· ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ· Ⲛ ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ϯⲛⲁⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ϯⲛⲁϫⲓ̈ ⲉⲟⲟⲩ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ϯⲛⲁϫⲓ̈ⲥⲉ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲍⲛⲍⲁⲛⲁⲩ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ⲧⲉⲧⲛϫⲍⲛⲝⲁϫⲓ ϣⲓ̈ⲡⲉ· ⲧϭⲟⲙ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲡ̅ⲛ̅ⲁ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲥϣⲟⲩⲉⲓⲧ· ⲡⲕⲱϩ̀ⲧ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲉⲙ ⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̅· '2 ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛϩⲉⲑⲛⲟⲥ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲉⲩⲣⲟⲕϩ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩϣⲟⲛⲧⲉ ⲉⲥⲛⲏϫ ϩⲛ̅ ⲧⲥⲱϣⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲥⲣⲟⲕϩ· ⲛⲉⲧϩⲙ ⲡⲟⲩⲉ̅ ⲛⲁⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲉⲛⲉⲛⲧⲁⲓⲁⲁⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉⲧϩⲏⲛ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲁⲉⲓⲙⲉ ⲉⲧⲁϭⲟⲙ· ⲁⲛⲁⲛⲟⲙⲟⲥ ⲉⲧϩⲛ̅ ⲥⲓ̈ⲱⲛ ⲗⲟ· ⲡⲉⲥⲧⲱⲧ ⲛⲁϫⲓ ⲛⲛⲁⲥⲉⲃⲏⲥ· ⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ̅ ϫⲉ ⲟⲩⲛ̅ ⲕⲱϩⲧ ⲛⲁⲙⲟⲩϩ· ⲛⲓ̈ⲙ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲧⲁⲙⲱⲧⲛ̅ ⲉⲡⲙⲁ ⲛϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ· ⲡⲉⲧⲙⲟⲟϣⲉ ϩⲛ ⲧⲇⲓ̈ⲕⲁⲓ̈ⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ· ⲉⲧϫⲱ ⲛⲧⲉϩⲓ̈ⲏ ⲉⲧⲥⲟⲩⲧⲱⲛ· ⲉⲧⲙⲟⲥⲧⲉ ⲛⲧⲁⲛⲟⲙⲓ̈ⲁ‐· ⲙⲛ ⲡϫⲓ̈ ⲛϭⲟⲛ̅ⲥ̅· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϥⲛⲟⲩϩⲉ ⲛⲛⲉϥϭⲓϫ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ̅ ⲛⲇⲱⲣⲟⲛ· ⲉϥϩⲣⲟϣ ⲛⲛⲉϥⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ϫⲉ ⲛⲛⲉϥⲥⲱⲧⲙ̅ ⲉⲩϩⲁⲡ ⲛ̅ϫⲓ̈ ⲛϭⲟⲛⲥ· ⲉϥϣⲧⲁⲙ ⲛⲛⲉϥⲃⲁⲗ ϫⲉ ⲛⲛⲉϥⲛⲁⲩ ⲉϫⲓ̈ ⲛϭⲟⲛⲥ· ⲡⲁⲓ̅ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱϩ ϩⲙ ⲡⲙⲁ ⲛϣⲱ{ⲱ}ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧϫⲟⲥⲉ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲧⲡⲉⲧⲣⲁ ⲉⲧⲧⲁϫⲣⲏⲩ· ⲥⲉⲛⲁϯ ⲛⲁϥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲟⲉⲓⲕ·
seed of those who are unbelieving come to destruction! But our salvation is in their time of affliction. 3 Because of the voice of the fear of you, peoples were astonished for fear of you, and the nations scattered. 4 But now your spoils — of small and of great — will be gathered. As someone gathers grasshoppers, so will they mock you. 5 God who dwells in lofty places is holy; Sion has been filled with judgment and righteousness. 6 By the law they will be handed over. Our salvation is in the treasures: the wisdom and the knowledge and the piety come toward the Lord; these are the treasures of the righteousness. 7 See now! They themselves will be afraid with fear of you; those you feared will cry, entreating for peace because of you, for messengers will be sent, weeping bitterly, entreating for peace. 8 For the roads of these (people) will be made desolate; the fear of the nations has ceased, and the righteousness with these is being put away, and you will not reckon them to be men. 9 The land mourned; Lebanon was ashamed; Astaron became marshes; Galilee and Carmel will become visible. 10 “Now | will arise,” says the Lord, “now | will be glorified; now | will be exalted. 11 Now you will see: now you will be ashamed; the strength of your spirit will be vain; the fire will consume you. 12 And the nations will be burned like a thorn cast out in the field / and burned.” 13 Those who are far away will hear the things | have done; and those who are near will know my might. 14 The lawless in Sion have gone away; trembling will seize the impious. Who will declare to you that a fire is burning? Who will declare to you the everlasting place? 15 One walking in righteousness, speaking a straight way, hating lawlessness and unrighteousness and shaking off his hands from gifts, making his ears dull, lest he hear an unjust judgment, shutting his eyes, lest he see unrighteousness — 16 this one will live in the high dwelling place of the strong rock; the bread will


[p. 626] {#po.57.p626}
ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϥⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲛϩⲟⲧ· “ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲩⲣⲣⲟ ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲉⲟⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲃⲁⲗ ⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲩⲕⲁϩ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲉ· ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲛⲁⲙⲉⲗⲉⲧⲁ ⲛⲑⲟⲧⲉ ⲙⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ· “ ⲉⲩⲧⲱⲛ ⲛⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲅⲣⲁⲙⲙⲁⲧⲉⲩⲥ · ⲉⲩⲧⲱⲛ ⲛⲉⲧⲛ̅ϫⲓ̅ ϣⲟϫⲛⲉ· ⲉϥⲧⲱⲛ ⲡⲉⲧⲱⲡ ⲛⲛ̅ⲥⲓ̈ⲟⲩ· ⲟⲩⲕⲟⲩⲓ ⲙⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ· ⲡⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲙⲡⲟⲩϫⲓ̈ ϣⲟϫⲛⲉ ⲛⲁϥ· ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥⲥⲟⲩⲛ ⲛϩⲁϭⲃ ⲥⲙⲏ ϩⲱⲥⲧⲉ ⲉⲧⲙ̅ⲥⲱⲧⲙ· ⲟⲩⲗⲁⲟⲥ ⲉϥⲥⲟϣϥ̅· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙⲛ̅ ⲙⲛ̅ⲧⲣ̅ⲙ̅ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲥⲱⲧⲙ̅· ⲉⲓⲥ ⲧⲡⲟⲗⲓⲥ ⲥⲓ̈ⲱⲛ· ⲛⲟⲩⲃⲁⲗ ⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲡⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ̈· ⲓ̅ⲉⲣⲟⲩⲥⲁⲗⲏⲙ· ⲧⲡⲟⲗⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ⲣ̅ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲟ ⲛⲛⲉⲩⲕⲓ̈ⲙ ⲛ̅ϭⲓ ⲛⲟⲩⲥⲕⲩⲛⲏ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛⲛⲉϫⲛⲍⲛⲁⲉⲓⲱ ⲛⲛⲟⲩⲥⲕⲩⲛⲏ ⲕⲓ̈ⲙ ϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ ⲛⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓϣ· ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩϩ ⲥⲱⲗⲡ· ϫⲉ ⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ̅ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲣⲁⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈̅ⲥ· ⲟⲩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲙⲁ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ̅· ⲛⲓ̈ⲉⲣⲱⲟⲩ ⲙⲛ ⲛⲉⲓⲟⲟⲣ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱϣⲥ· ⲛⲥⲉⲟⲩⲟⲥⲧⲛ̅· ⲛ̅ⲛⲉⲃⲱⲕ ϩⲓ̈ ⲧⲉϩⲓ̈ⲏ· ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲟⲩϫⲟⲓ̈ ⲙⲟⲟϣⲉ ⲉϥϩⲓ̈ⲛⲉ· ⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲁⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥⲥⲟⲟⲧ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉⲛⲉⲓⲱⲧ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉⲛⲣⲉϥϯ ϩⲁⲡ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉⲛⲁⲣⲭⲱⲛ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉⲛⲣⲣⲟ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲟϥ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲁⲧⲁⲛϩⲟⲛ· ⲁⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩϩ ⲥⲱⲗ̅ⲡ ϫⲉ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲡⲟⲩϣⲧⲏ ϭⲙϭⲟⲙ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥⲣⲓⲕⲉ· ⲛⲛⲟⲩⲗⲁⲩⲟ ⲛⲁⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲁⲛ· ⲛ̅ϥ̅ⲛⲁϥⲓ̈ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲟⲩⲙⲁⲉⲓⲛ ϣⲁⲛⲧⲟⲩⲧⲁⲁⲥ ⲉⲩϣⲱⲗ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲛ̅ ϩⲁϩ ⲛ̅ϭⲁⲗⲉ ⲛⲁⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲛⲟⲩϣⲱⲗ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲛⲉⲩϫⲟⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲧⲛ̅ϩⲟⲥⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲛⲉⲡⲉⲓⲗⲁⲟⲥ ϫⲟⲟⲥ
be given to him, and his water (will be) assured. 17 You will see a king with glory, and your eyes will see a land from far away. 18 Your soul will muse on the fear of the Lord: “Where are your secretaries? Where are your counselors? Where is the one who counts the stars, 19 a small and a great one?” They took no counsel with him, nor did he know deep speech, so that a despised people could not hear, and there is no understanding to the one who hears. 20 Look, the city of Sion! Your eyes will see the salvation. lerousalem a wealthy city: your tents will not be shaken, nor will the stakes of your tents be moved forever, nor will your ropes be broken. 21 Because the name of the Lord is great to you, there will be a place for you — rivers and canals will be broad and spacious. You shall not go this way, nor shall a ship go sailing. 22 For my God is great; and he will not pass me by. The Lord is our father, the Lord is our judge; the Lord is our ruler; the Lord is our king; the Lord — it is he who will save us. 23 Your ropes broke, because your mast was not strong and it has bent; it will not release your sails; it will not raise a signal until it is given up for plunder; therefore many lame will take plunder. 24 And they will not say, “We are weary”. And these people who dwell in it will not say it for their sin has been forgiven.

34 1 Draw near to me, O nations, and hear, O rulers! Let the earth hear, and those who dwell in it, the world, and its people, 2 because the wrath of the Lord comes against all the nations and (his) anger against the (whole) number of them, to destroy them and to give them over for slaughter. 3 Their slain and their dead shall be cast out, and their stench shall rise; and the mountains shall be drenched with their blood, 4 and heaven shall roll up like a scroll, and all the stars shall fall like leaves of twig from a vine and as leaves fall from a fig tree. 5 My dagger has become drunk in heaven; lo, it will descend upon Idumea and upon the people of destruction with judgment. 6 The dagger of the Lord is filled with blood; it is gorged with the fat of the lambs and the fat of the goats and rams, because the Lord has a sacrifice in Bosor and a great slaughter in Idumea. 7 And the goats shall fall with them, (both) with the rams. and the bulls. And the land shall become drunk with their blood, and it shall be filled with their fat. 8 For (it is) the day of the Lord’s judgment; and the year of recompense for Sion has come upon them. 9 And her ravines shall be turned into pitch, and her land into sulfur; her land shall burn like pitch 10 night and day, and it shall not be quenched forever, and its smoke shall go up above; for generations it shall be made desolate and for a long time it shall be made desolate. 11 And birds shall dwell in it with the vipers and the ibises and the ravens. A measuring line of desolation shall be cast over it, and donkey-centaurs shall dwell in it. 12 Her rulers shall not be, for her kings and her rulers shall be destroyed. 13 Thorn trees shall grow up in her cities and (in) her

[p. 630] {#po.57.p630}
ϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ϩⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲥⲡⲟⲗⲓ̈ⲥ· ⲙⲛ ⲛⲉⲥⲙⲁ ⲉⲧϫⲟⲥⲉ· ⲛ̅ⲥϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲛⲉⲣ̅ⲥⲱ ⲛⲛⲥⲓ̈ⲣⲏⲛⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲟϩⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲥⲧⲣⲟⲩⲑⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲇⲁⲓ̈ⲙⲟⲛⲓⲟⲛ ⲛⲁⲧⲱⲙⲛⲧ ⲉⲛϩⲟⲛⲟⲕⲉⲛⲧⲁⲩⲣⲟⲥ· ⲛⲥⲉϫⲓ ϣⲕⲁⲕ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ· ⲟⲩⲁ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲁ· ⲉⲣⲉⲛϩⲟⲛⲟⲕⲉⲛⲧⲁⲩⲣⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲙⲧⲟⲛ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ· ⲁⲩϩⲉ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲛⲉⲥϣⲏⲣⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲡⲕⲁϩ ⲧⲟⲩϫⲟ ⲛⲛⲉⲥϣⲏⲣⲉ ϩⲛ ⲟⲩⲱⲣϫ· ⲛⲧⲁⲛⲓ̈ⲟⲩⲗ ⲧⲱⲙⲛⲧ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ· ⲁⲩⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲡϩⲟ ⲛⲛ{ⲛ}ⲉⲩⲉⲣⲏⲩ· ⲁⲩⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲏⲡⲉ ⲙⲡⲉⲟⲩⲁ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲥⲱⲣⲙ̅· ⲙⲡⲉⲟⲩⲁ ϣⲓⲛⲉ ⲛⲥⲁ ⲟⲩⲁ· ϫⲉ ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥϩⲱⲛ ⲉⲧⲟⲟⲧⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϥⲡⲛⲁ ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥϩⲟⲟⲩϩⲟⲩ'·'. ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲟϥ ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲩ ⲛϩⲛ̅ⲕⲗⲏⲣⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲧⲉϥϭⲓϫ ⲧⲉⲛⲧⲁⲥⲡⲱϣ ⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲙⲟⲟⲛⲉ ϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ ⲛⲟⲩⲟⲉⲓϣ ⲛⲍⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅‐ⲕⲗⲏⲣⲟⲛⲟⲙⲉⲓ ⲛ̅ϩⲉⲛϫⲱⲙ ⲛ̅ϫⲱⲙ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϫⲥⲉϩⲛⲁⲙⲧⲟⲛ
fortresses. And it shall be a habitation of sirens and a courtyard of the ostriches. 14 And demons shall meet with donkey-centaurs and call one to another; there donkey-centaurs shall repose, for they have found for themselves a place to rest. 15 There has the viper nourished her youths, and the earth has preserved her young in safety; there the harts met and saw each other's face. 16 They have passed by in number, and not one of them has perished; they did not seek each other, because the Lord has commanded them and his spirit has gathered them. 17 He will himself cast lots for them; and his hand has distributed (food) for them to graze forever. You will inherit (the land), for generations of generations. And they shall rest on it.
ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲉϫⲱⲥ· ⲉⲩⲫⲣⲁⲛⲉ ⲧⲉⲣⲏⲙⲟⲥ ⲉⲧⲟⲃⲉ· ⲙⲁⲣⲉⲧⲉⲣⲏⲙⲟⲥ ⲧⲉⲗⲏⲗ· ⲛⲥϯ ⲟⲩⲱ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲕⲣⲓ̈ⲛⲟⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛϫⲁⲓ̈ⲉ ⲙⲡⲓ̈ⲟⲣⲇⲁⲛⲏⲥ ⲛⲁϯ ⲟⲩⲱ ⲛⲥⲉⲧⲉⲗⲏⲗ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲩϯ ⲛⲁⲥ ⲙⲡⲉⲟⲟⲩ ⲙⲡⲗⲓ̈ⲃⲁⲛⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲧⲁⲓ̈ⲟ ⲙⲡⲕⲁⲣⲙⲏⲗⲟⲥ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲁⲗⲁⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲡⲉⲟⲟⲩ ⲙⲡϫⲟⲉⲓⲥ· ⲙⲛ ⲡϫⲓⲥⲉ ⲙⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ· ϭⲙϭⲟⲙ ⲛϭⲓϫ ⲉⲧⲥⲏϭ ⲙⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲧ ⲉⲧⲃⲏⲗ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ· “ ⲡⲁⲣⲁⲕⲁⲗⲉⲓ ⲛ̅ⲛϩⲏⲧ ϣⲏⲙ ϩⲙ ⲡⲉⲩϩⲏⲧ· ⲙⲡⲉⲣⲣ̅ ϩⲟⲧⲉ· ⲉⲓⲥ ϩⲏⲏⲧⲉ ⲡⲉⲛⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲧⲱⲱⲃⲉ ϩⲉⲛ ⲟⲩϩⲁⲡ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϥⲛⲁⲧⲱⲱⲃⲉ ⲛⲁⲩ· ⲛⲧⲟϥ ⲡⲉⲧⲛⲏⲩ ⲛ̅ϥ̅ⲧⲟⲩϫⲟⲛ· ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲛⲃⲁⲗ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ⲃⲗⲗⲉ ⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲛⲁⲗ ⲛⲁⲥⲱⲧⲙ̅· ϭ ⲧⲟⲧⲉ ⲛϭⲁⲗⲉ ⲛⲁϫⲓ ⲃⲁϭⲥ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲛⲛⲓ̈ⲟⲩⲗ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲗⲁⲥ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ϭⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲗⲁⲥ ⲛⲁⲥⲟⲟⲩⲧⲛ̅ ϫⲉ ⲁⲩⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲥⲱⲕ ϩⲓ̈ ⲡϫⲁⲓ̈ⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲟⲩⲉⲓⲁ ϩⲛ ⲟⲩⲕⲁϩ ⲉϥⲟⲃⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲙⲛ ⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ϩⲏⲧϥ̅ ⲛⲁⲣ̅ ϩⲉⲗⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲟⲩⲡⲩⲅⲏ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲕⲁϩ ⲛⲉⲓⲃⲉ· ϥⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛϭⲓ ⲟⲩⲟⲩⲛⲟϥ ⲛϩⲁⲗⲏⲧ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϩⲛ̅ⲣ̅ⲥⲱ ⲛⲟϩⲉ· ⲟⲩⲛ̅ ϩⲉⲛⲕⲁϣ ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ϩⲛϩⲉⲗⲟⲥ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ· ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲙⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲉⲣⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲧⲉϩⲓ̈ⲏ ⲉⲧⲧ̅ⲃ̅ⲃⲏⲩ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲧⲟⲩⲁⲁⲃ· ⲛⲛⲉⲁⲕⲁⲑⲁⲣⲧⲟⲥ ⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓ̈ⲧⲙ̅ ⲡⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ· ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛⲛⲉϩⲓ̈ⲏ ⲛⲁⲕⲁⲑⲁⲣⲧⲟⲥ ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ‐· ⲛⲉⲧϫⲟⲟⲣⲉ ⲇⲉ
35 1 Rejoice, O thirsty wilderness! Let the wilderness be glad, and let it blossom like a lily! 2 And the deserted places of the Jordan shall blossom and be glad. And the glory of Lebanon has been given to it, and the honor of Carmel, and my people shall see the glory of the Lord and the loftiness of God. 3 Be strong, you weak hands and feeble knees! 4 Give comfort to those who are discouraged in their heart! Be strong; do not fear! Look, our God will repay with judgment; and he will repay them; he himself will come and save us. 5 Then the eyes of the blind shall be opened, and the ears of the deaf shall hear; 6 then the lame shall leap like deers, and the tongue of stammerers shall be clear, because water has flowed in the wilderness and a gully in a thirsty land; 7 the dry place shall turn into marshlands, and in the thirsty land there (shall be) a spring of water; (the) joy of birds shall be there and folds of flocks; reeds and marshlands shall be there. 8 It shall be called: the pure and holy way; and (the) unclean shall not pass by there, nor shall be there an unclean way, but those who have been dispersed shall walk on it,


[p. 632] {#po.57.p632}
ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁⲙⲟⲟϣⲉ ϩⲓ̈ϫⲱϥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ⲛⲉⲩⲡⲗⲁⲛⲁ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ⲛⲉⲙⲟⲩⲓ̈ ϣⲱⲡⲉ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ· ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛ̅ⲛⲉⲗⲁⲁⲩ ⲛⲑⲏⲣⲓ̈ⲱⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲟⲛⲏⲣⲟⲛ ⲁⲗⲉ ⲉϫⲱϥ· ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛ̅ⲛⲉⲩϩⲉ ⲉⲣⲟⲟⲩ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ· ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲙⲟⲟϣⲉ ⲛϩⲏⲧⲥ ⲉⲁⲩⲥⲟⲧⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲩⲥⲟⲟⲩϩ· ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲕⲟⲧⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲃⲉ ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲥⲉⲉⲓ ⲉⲥⲓ̈ⲱⲛ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲟⲩⲛⲟϥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲣⲉⲡⲟⲩⲛⲟϥ ⲛϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ ϩⲓ̈ϫⲛ̅ ⲧⲉⲩⲁⲡⲉ· ⲡⲉⲥⲙⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲓ̈ϫⲱⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲟⲩⲛⲟϥ ⲙⲛ̅ ⲡⲧⲉⲗⲏⲗ ⲛⲁⲧⲁϩⲟⲟⲩ· ⲁⲧⲗⲩⲡⲏ ⲡⲱⲧ ⲙⲛ̅ ⲡⲉⲙⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ·
and they shall not go astray. 9 And no lion shall be there, nor shall any of the evil beasts come up on it or be found there, but the redeemed shall walk on it. 10 And (those) gathered together, they shall return / because of the Lord and come to Sion with joy; and the everlasting joy (shall be) above their head; for upon their head shall be the praise. And gladness with joy shall take hold of them — the sorrow / and the pain and the sighing have fled away.
ⲙⲛ̅ ⲡⲁϣ ⲁϩⲟⲙ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲥϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲛ̅ ⲧⲙⲉϩⲙⲛⲧⲁϥⲧⲉ ⲛⲣⲟⲙⲡⲉ ⲉⲣⲉⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓⲁⲥ ⲟ ⲛ̅ⲣ̅ⲣⲟ· ⲁⲥⲉⲛⲁⲭⲁⲣⲉⲓⲙ ⲉⲓ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛ̅ⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓ̀ⲟⲥ ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲛ̅ⲡⲟⲗⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛϯⲟⲩⲇⲁⲓ̈ⲁ ⲉⲧϫⲟⲥⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥϫⲓ̈ⲧⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥϫⲟⲟⲩ ⲛϭⲓ ⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛ̅ⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓⲟⲥ ⲛϩⲣⲁⲯⲁⲅⲏⲥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ̅ ⲗⲁⲭⲓ̈ⲥ ⲉⲓⲉⲣⲟⲩⲥⲁⲗⲏⲙ ϣⲁ ⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ⲡⲉⲣⲟ ⲙⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛϭⲟⲙ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥⲁϩⲉ ⲣⲁⲧϥ ϩⲛ̅ ⲧϥ̅ⲟ ⲛⲧⲕⲗⲩⲙⲃⲏⲑⲣⲁ· ⲉⲧϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ϩⲛ̅ ⲧⲉϩⲓ̈ⲓ ⲛⲧⲥⲱϣⲉ ⲙ̅ⲡⲣⲁϩ̅ⲧ̅· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲉⲗⲓ̈ⲁⲕⲓ̈ⲙ ⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁϥ ⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲛⲭⲉⲗⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ⲡⲟⲓ̈ⲕⲟⲛⲟⲙⲟⲥ ⲙⲛ̅ ⲥⲟⲙⲛⲁⲥ ⲡⲉⲅⲣⲁⲙⲙⲁⲧⲉⲩⲥ· ⲙⲛ̅ ⲓ̈ⲱⲁⲭⲁⲥ ⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲛⲁⲥⲁⲫ ⲡϩⲩⲡⲟⲙⲛⲏⲙⲁⲧⲟⲅⲣⲁⲫⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϫⲉ ϩⲣⲁⲯⲁⲅⲏⲥ ⲛⲁⲩ· ϫⲉ ⲁϫⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲓ̈ ⲛⲉⲧⲉⲣⲉⲡⲛⲟϭ ⲛ̅ⲣ̅ⲣⲟ ϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛ̅ⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓ̈ⲟⲥ· ϫⲉ ⲉⲣⲉϩⲧⲏⲕ ⲕⲏ ⲉⲛⲓⲙ· ⲙⲏ ϣⲁⲣⲉⲙ̅ⲗⲁϩ ⲥⲱⲣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ̅ ⲟⲩϣⲟϫⲛⲉ· ϩⲓ̈ ϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛ̅ⲥⲡⲟⲧⲟⲩ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ϭⲉ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲕⲕⲁ ϩⲧⲏⲕ ⲉⲛⲓ̈ⲙ· ϫⲉ ⲛ̅ⲅⲥⲱⲧⲙ̅ ⲛⲁⲓ̈ ⲁⲛ· ⲉⲓⲥ ϩⲏⲏⲧⲉ ⲉⲕⲛⲁϩⲧⲉ ⲉϫⲙ̅ ⲡϭⲉⲣⲱⲃ ⲛⲕⲁϣ ⲉⲧⲡⲟⲧⲥ ⲉϫⲛ̅ ⲕⲏⲙⲉ· ⲡⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲧⲉ ϣⲁⲣⲉⲟⲩⲁ ⲧⲁϫⲣⲟϥ ⲉϫⲱϥ· ϥⲛⲁⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲧⲉϥϭⲓϫ· ⲧⲁⲓ̈ ⲧⲉ ⲑⲉ ⲙ̅ⲫⲁⲣⲁⲱ ⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛ̅ⲕⲏⲙⲉ· ⲙⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲉⲧⲛⲁϩⲧⲉ ⲉⲣⲟϥ· ⲉϣϫⲉ ⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅ϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲛⲛⲁϩⲧⲉ ⲉⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲡⲉⲛⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ⲧⲱϩ ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲡⲉⲛϫⲟⲉⲓ̅ⲥ ⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛ̅ⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓ̈ⲟⲥ· ⲛ̅ⲧⲁϯ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ̅ ⲛ̅ϣⲟ ⲛϩⲧⲟ· ⲉϣϫⲉ ⲟⲩⲛ̅ ϣϭⲟⲙ ⲙ̅ⲙⲱⲧⲛ̅ ⲉϯ ⲣⲉϥⲁⲗⲉ ⲉϫⲱⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲁϣ ⲛϩⲉ ⲧⲉⲧⲛⲁϣϭⲙ̅ ϭⲟⲙ ⲉϭⲱϣⲧ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡϩⲟ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ⲧⲟⲡⲁⲣⲭⲏⲥ· ϩⲛ̅ ϩⲙϩⲁⲗ ⲛⲉⲧⲕⲱ ⲛϩⲧⲏⲩ ⲉⲛⲣⲙ̅ⲛ̅ⲕⲏⲙⲉ·
36 1 And it happened in the fourteenth year of the reign of Hezekias that Sennacherim, the king of the Assyrians, came up against the strong cities of Judea and took them. 2 The king of the Assyrians sent Rapsakes from Lachis to lerousalem, to King Hezekias, with a great force. And he stood by the conduit of the upper pool in the way of the Fuller's Field. 3 And there went out to him Eliakim the son of Chelkias, the steward, and Somnas, the secretary, and loachas the son of Asaph, the recorder. 4 And Rapsakes said to them, “Say to Hezekias: This is what the great king, the king of the Assyrians, says: Whom are you trusting? 5 Does marshalling take place through counsel or by words of the lips? But now in whom do you trust, that you are rebelling against me? 6 See, you are trusting in the broken reed-rod, in Egypt; whoever leans on it, it will go into his hand. Such is Pharao, king of Egypt, and everybody who trusts in him. 7 (But) if you say, ‘We trust in the Lord our God’, 8 come in contact now with our lord the king of the Assyrians. | will give you thousand horses, if you will be able to set riders on them. 9 How then will you be able to turn back to the face of the governors? Those who trust in Egyptians for horse and rider are domestics.


[p. 634] {#po.57.p634}
ⲉⲩϩⲧⲟ ⲙⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲁⲛ̅ⲃⲁⲧⲏⲥ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ϭⲉ ⲙⲏ ⲁϫⲙ̅ ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲓⲉⲓ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲧⲉⲓⲭⲱⲣⲁ ⲉⲙⲓϣⲉ ⲛ̅ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲥ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓⲥ ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲛⲁⲓ̈ ϫⲉ ⲃⲱⲕ ⲛ̅ⲅ̅ⲧⲁⲕⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲉⲗⲓⲁⲕⲓⲙ ⲛⲁϥ ⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲛⲭⲉⲗⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ⲡⲟⲓⲕⲟⲛⲟⲙⲟⲥ· ⲙⲛ̅ ⲥⲟⲙⲛⲁⲥ ⲡⲉⲅⲣⲁⲙⲙⲁⲧⲉⲩⲥ· ϫⲉ ϣⲁϫⲉ ⲙⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲕϩⲙ̅ϩⲁⲗ ⲙ̅ⲙⲛ̅ⲧⲥⲩⲣⲟⲥ ⲧⲛ̅ⲥⲱⲧⲙ̅ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲁⲛⲟⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙ̅ⲡⲣ̅ϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛ̅ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲛ ϩⲙ̅ⲙⲛ̅ⲧⲓ̈ⲟⲩⲇⲁⲓ̈· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲧⲃⲉ ⲟⲩ ⲕϣⲁϫⲉ ⲉⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲁϫⲉ ⲛ̅ⲛⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲉⲧϩⲓ̈ϫⲙ̅ ⲡⲥⲟⲃⲧ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϫⲉ ϩⲣⲁⲯⲁⲅⲏⲥ ⲛⲁⲩ· ϫⲉ ⲙⲏ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲡⲁϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲧⲛ̅ⲛⲟⲟⲩⲧ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲧⲛ̅ϫⲟⲉⲓ̅ⲥ· ⲏ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ̅ ⲙⲏ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁϥⲧⲛ̅ⲛⲟⲟⲩⲧ ⲁⲛ̅ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ⲣⲱⲙⲉ ⲉⲧϩⲓ̈ϫⲙ̅ ⲡⲥⲟⲃⲧ· ϫⲉⲕⲁⲥ ⲉⲩⲉⲟⲩⲱⲙ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲩⲙⲏ ⲟⲉⲓⲕ· ⲛ̅ⲥⲉⲥ ⲧⲉⲩⲙⲏ ⲍⲙϩⲙⲟⲟⲩ ϩⲓ̈ ⲟⲩⲥⲟⲡ ⲛⲙⲙⲏⲧⲛ̅· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϩⲣⲁⲯⲁⲅⲏⲥ ⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲁϥⲱϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ ⲟⲩⲛⲟϭ ⲛⲥⲙⲏ ⲙⲙⲛⲧⲓ̈ⲟⲩⲇⲁⲓ̈· ϫⲉ ⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲉⲛϣⲁϫⲉ ⲙⲡⲛⲟϭ ⲛⲣⲣⲟ ⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲍⲛⲍⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓ̈ⲟⲥ· ⲛⲁⲓ̈ ⲛⲉⲧⲉⲣⲉⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓⲟⲥ ϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲟⲩ· ϫⲉ ⲙⲡⲣⲧⲣⲉⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ⲁⲡⲁⲧⲁ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ ϩⲛ̅ ϩⲉⲛϣⲁϫⲉ ⲉⲙⲛ ϭⲟⲙ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̅· '5 ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙ̅ⲡⲣ̅ⲧⲣⲉⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ϫⲟⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲧⲛ̅ ϫⲉ ⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲛⲉϩⲙ̅ ⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϫⲉ ⲛ·ⲩ̀ⲛⲉⲩϯ ⲛⲧⲉⲓⲡⲟⲗⲓⲥ ⲉⲛⲉϭⲓϫ ⲙⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓ̈ⲟⲥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲣ̅ⲥⲱⲧⲙ̅ ⲛⲥⲁ ⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓⲁⲥ· ⲛⲁⲓ̈ ⲛⲉⲧⲉⲣⲉⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲍⲛⲍⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓ̈ⲟⲥ ϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲟⲩ· ϫⲉ ⲉϣϫⲉ ⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲉϫⲓ̈ ⲥⲙⲟⲩ ⲁⲙⲏⲓ̈ⲧⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲣⲁⲧ· ⲛⲧⲉⲡⲟⲩⲁ ⲡⲟⲩⲁ ⲟⲩⲱⲙ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉϥⲃⲱ ⲛⲉⲗⲟⲟⲗⲉ· ⲙⲛ ⲧⲉϥⲃⲱ ⲛⲕⲛ̅ⲧⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲉⲧⲛ̅ⲥⲉ ⲙⲟⲟⲩ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡⲉⲧⲛ̅ϣⲏⲓ̈· Ⲙ ϣⲁⲛϯⲉⲓ ⲧⲁϫⲓ ⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ̅ ⲉⲩⲕⲁϩ ⲛ̅ⲑⲉ ⲙⲡⲉⲧⲛⲕⲁϩ· ⲟⲩⲕⲁϩ ⲛⲥⲟⲩⲟ· ϩⲓ̈ ⲏⲣⲡ ϩⲓ̈ ⲟⲉⲓⲕ· ϩⲓ̈ ⲙⲁ ⲛⲉⲗⲟⲟⲗⲉ· ' ⲙⲡⲣⲧⲣⲉⲉⲍⲉⲕⲍⲓϩⲁⲥ ⲁⲡⲁⲧⲁ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲛ ⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲛⲉϩⲙ̅ ⲧⲏⲩⲧⲛ· ⲙⲏ ⲁⲡⲟⲩⲁ ⲡⲟⲩⲁ ⲛ̅ⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ϩⲉⲑⲛⲟⲥ ⲛⲉϩⲙ̅ ⲧⲉϥⲭⲱⲣⲁ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓ̈ⲧⲙ ⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓ̈ⲟⲥ Ⲙ ⲉϥⲧⲱⲛ ⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲙⲁⲣ ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲁⲣⲫⲁⲇ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉϥⲧⲱⲛ
10 And now, is it without the Lord that | have come up against this country to war against it?” It is the Lord, who told me: “Go and destroy it’! 11 Then Eliakim the son of Chelkias, the steward and Somnas the secretary, said to him, “Speak with your servants in Syrian, for we understand (it); and do not speak with us in Judean. And why do you speak to the ears of the people who are on the wall?” 12 And Rapsakes said to them, “Has my lord sent me to your lord or to you? Has he sent me not to the people (sitting) on the wall so that they may eat their dung and drink their urine together with you?” 13 Then Rapsakes stood and called out in a loud voice in Judean: “Hear the words of the great king, the king of the Assyrians! 14 This is what the king of the Assyrians says: ‘Do not let Hezekias deceive you with words that are not able to deliver you. 15 And do not let Hezekias say to you, God will deliver you, and this city will not be given up into the hands of the king of the Assyrians.’ 16 Do not listen to Hezekias. This is what the king of the Assyrians says: ‘If you wish to be blessed, come out to me; then everyone of you will eat from his (own) vine and his (own) fig tree and you will drink water from your (own) cistern, 17 until | come and take you to a land like your land, a land of grain and wine and bread and vineyard. 18 Do not let Hezekias deceive you by saying: ‘God will deliver you’. Has each one of the gods of the nations delivered his (own) country out of the king of the Assyrians? 19 Where is the god of Amar and Arphad? And where is the god


[p. 636] {#po.57.p636}
ⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲧⲡⲟⲗⲓ̈ⲥ ⲛⲥⲁⲫⲁⲣⲉⲙ· ⲙⲏ ⲁⲩⲉϣ ϭⲙϭⲟⲙ ⲉⲛⲟⲩϩⲙ̅ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲟⲩ· ⲏ ⲉⲛⲉϩⲙ̅ ⲧⲥⲁⲙⲁⲣⲓⲁ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ ⲧⲁϭⲓϫ· ⲛⲓⲙ ⲛⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ϩⲉⲑⲛⲟⲥ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲡⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲛⲉϩⲙ ⲡⲉϥⲕⲁϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲛ̅ ⲧⲁϭⲓϫ· ϫⲉ ⲉⲣⲉⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲛⲉϩⲙ̅ ⲑ̅ⲓ̅ⲗ̅ⲏ̅ⲙ̅ ⲉⲧⲁϭⲓϫ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲩⲕⲁ ⲣⲱⲟⲩ ⲙⲡⲉⲗⲁⲁⲩ ⲟⲩⲱϣⲃ̅ ⲛⲁϥ ⲛⲟⲩϣⲁϫⲉ· ⲉⲧⲃⲉ ϫⲉ ⲁⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲟⲩⲉϩ ⲥⲁϩⲛⲉ ⲉⲧⲙ̅ⲧⲣⲉⲗⲁⲁⲩ ⲟⲩⲱϣⲃ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲉⲛⲗⲓ̈ⲁⲕⲓ̈ⲙ ⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲛⲭⲉⲗⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ· ⲡⲟⲓⲕⲟⲛⲟⲙⲟⲥ· ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲥⲟⲙⲛⲁⲥ ⲡⲉⲅⲣⲁⲙⲁⲧⲉⲩⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧϭⲟⲙ· ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲁⲭⲁⲍ̅ ⲡϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲛⲁⲥⲁⲫ ⲡϩⲩⲡⲟⲙⲛⲏⲙⲁⲧⲟⲅⲣⲁⲫⲟⲥ ϣⲁ ⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ⲉⲣⲉⲛⲉⲩϩⲟⲓⲧⲉ
of the city of Sapharem? Have they been able to deliver them or to deliver Samaria out of my hand? 20 Which of the gods of all the nations has delivered his land out of my hand, that God should deliver lerousalem out of my hand?” 21 And they were silent, and no one answered him a word, because the king had ordered that no one should answer. 22 And Eliakim the son of Chelkias, the steward, and Somnas, the secretary of the army, and Achaz the son of Asaph, the recorder, came to Hezekias with their tunics torn and reported to him the words of Rapsakes.

37 1 Then it happened, when Hezekias heard the words of Rapsakes, (that) he tore his clothes, put sackcloth about him and went up into the house of the Lord. 2 And he sent Eliakim the steward and Somnas, the secretary, and the senior priests, with sackcloth about them, to the prophet Esaias the son of Amos. 3 They said to him, “This is what Hezekias says, Today is a day of affliction and of reproach and of rebuke and of anger, because the pangs have come to the woman giving birth but she has no strength to give birth. 4 May the Lord, your God, listen to the words of Rapsakes, whom the king of the Assyrians has sent to revile the living God, and to revile with (the) words that the Lord, your God, has heard; and you will pray to the Lord your God concerning these that are left.” 5 And the servants of the king came to Esaias, 6 and Esaias said to them, “Thus you will say to your lord, ‘This is what the Lord says: Do not be afraid because of the words that you have heard, with which the ambassadors of the king of the Assyrians have reviled me. 7 Look, | will put a spirit in him, and he will hear a report, and he will return to his country, and he will fall by a dagger in his (own) land.”


[p. 638] {#po.57.p638}

8 And Rapsakes returned and found the king besieging Lomna. 9 And the king of the Assyrians heard that Saratha, the king of the Ethiopians, had gone out to besiege him. And he heard (it), he turned back and sent messengers to Hezekias, saying, 10 “Thus shall you speak to Hezekias the king of Judea: Do not let your God in whom you trust deceive you, by saying, lerousalem will not be given over into the hands of the king of the Assyrians. 11 Or have you not heard the things that the kings of the Assyrians have done to the entire earth, how they have destroyed it? 12 Have the gods of the nations delivered them whom my fathers destroyed, both Gozate and Charran and Raphessa, which are in the country of Thaimman? 13 Where are the kings of Amar and where is Arphad and where is Hanag Ougaua of Seppharim — the city?” 14 And Hezekias received the letter from the messengers and opened the letter before the Lord. 15 And Hezekias prayed to God, saying: 16 “O Lord Sabaoth, God of Israel, who sits upon the cheroubin, you alone are God and the Lord of all kingdoms of the world; you have made heaven and earth. 17 Listen, O Lord; look, O Lord, and see the words that Sennacherim — the king of the Assyrians — has sent to revile the living God. 18 For truly the kings of the Assyrians have made desolate the whole world and their country 19 and have hurled their idols into the fire, for they were no gods, but they are the works of human hands — they are wood and stones — and they destroyed them. 20 But now, O Lord, our God, save us from their hands so that every kingdom of the earth may know that you alone are God.” 21 Then Esaias the son of Amos was sent to Hezekias and said to him: “This is what

ϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ· ϫⲉ ⲁⲓⲥⲱⲧⲙ ⲉⲛⲉⲕϣⲗⲏⲗ ⲛⲧⲁⲕⲁⲁⲩ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ̈ ⲉⲧⲃⲉ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲭⲁⲣⲉⲓⲙ ⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛⲛⲁⲥⲥⲩⲣⲓⲟⲥ. ⲡⲁⲓ ⲡⲉ ⲡϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲧⲁⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ϫⲟⲟϥ ⲉⲧⲃⲏⲏⲧϥ̅· ϫⲉ ⲁϥⲥⲟϣⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥⲕⲟⲙϣⲉ ⲧⲡⲁⲣⲑⲉⲛⲟⲥ ⲧϣⲉⲉⲣⲉ ⲛⲥⲓ̈ⲱⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥⲕⲓⲙ ⲛⲧⲉϥⲁⲡⲉ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲉϫⲱⲧ. ⲱ ⲧϣⲉⲉⲣⲉ ⲛⲓ̅ⲗ̅ⲏ̅ⲙ̅· ⲛⲧⲁⲕⲛⲉϭ ⲛⲓⲙ- ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲁⲕϯ ⲛⲟⲩϫⲱ ⲛⲛⲓ̈ⲙ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲕϫⲓ̈ ⲟⲩⲁ ⲉⲛⲓ̈ⲙ· ⲏ ⲛⲧⲁⲕϫⲉⲥⲧ ⲧⲉⲕϭⲓϫ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ ⲉⲛⲓⲙ‐ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲙⲡⲉⲕϥⲓ̈ ⲛⲛⲉⲕⲃⲁⲗ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲡⲉⲧⲟⲩⲁⲁⲃ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲏ̅ⲗ· ϫⲉ ⲁⲕⲛⲉϭⲛⲉϭ ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓⲥ ϩⲓ̈ⲧⲛ ϩⲉⲛⲃⲁⲓ̈ϣⲓ̈ⲛⲉ· ⲛⲧⲟⲕ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲁⲕϫⲟⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ϩⲙ̅ ⲡⲁϣⲁⲓ̈ ⲛⲛⲁϩⲁⲣⲙⲁ· ⲁⲓ̈ⲁⲗⲉ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲡϫⲓⲥⲉ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ⲧⲟⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲁⲣⲏϫϥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲗⲓ̈ⲃⲁⲛⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲓϣⲱⲱⲧ ⲙⲡϫⲓⲥⲉ ⲛⲧⲉϥⲕⲉⲇⲣⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲥⲁ ⲛⲧⲕⲩⲡⲁⲣⲓⲥⲟⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲓⲃⲱⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲡϫⲓ̈ⲥⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲥⲁ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲡⲉⲇⲣⲏⲙⲟⲥ· ⲁⲓⲕⲱ ⲛⲟⲩⲙⲁ ⲛⲉϫⲓ̈ⲟⲟⲣ· ⲁⲓⲉⲓⲣⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲙⲟⲟⲩ ⲛϫⲁⲓ̈ⲉ ⲁⲩⲱ ⲥⲩⲛⲁⲅⲱⲅⲏ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ· ⲛⲛⲁⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲉⲛⲧⲁⲕⲥⲟⲧⲙⲟⲩ ⲛϣⲟⲣⲡ· ⲛⲉⲛⲧⲁⲓⲁⲁⲩ ϫⲓⲛ ⲛⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲛϣⲟⲣⲡ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲓⲧⲟϣⲟⲩ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ⲇⲉ ⲁⲓϩⲓ ⲧⲟⲟⲧ ⲉⲣ ⲛϩⲉⲑⲛⲟⲥ ⲛϫⲁⲓ̈ⲉ· ⲉⲩⲛⲁϣⲧ· ⲉⲩϩⲉⲛ ϩⲛ̅ⲙⲁ ⲉⲩϫⲟⲟⲣ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲉⲩⲟⲩⲏϩ ϩⲉⲛ ϩⲛⲡⲟⲗⲓⲥ ⲉⲩϫⲟⲥⲉ· ⲁⲩⲕⲁ ⲧⲟⲟⲧⲟⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲩϣⲟⲟⲩⲉ· ⲉⲁⲩⲣ ⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲭⲟⲣⲧⲟⲥ ⲉϥϣⲟⲩⲱⲟⲩ ϩⲓ ϩⲉⲛϫⲉⲛⲉⲡⲱⲣ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲑⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲉⲛⲧⲏϭ· ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩ ⲇⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲙ̅ⲧⲟⲛ· ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲧⲉⲕϭⲓⲛⲉⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ· ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲧⲉⲕϭⲓⲛⲃⲱ̅ⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲉⲧⲥⲟⲟⲩⲛ ⲙⲙⲟⲟⲩ· ⲡⲉⲕϭⲱⲛⲧ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲛⲧⲁⲕϭⲱⲛⲧ ⲛϩⲏⲧϥ̅ ⲙⲛ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲟⲩϭⲥ ⲁϥⲉⲓ ⲉϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲛⲁⲓ· ⲁⲩⲱ ϯⲛⲁⲛⲟⲩϫⲉ ⲛⲟⲩϣⲧⲁϥ ⲉⲛⲉⲕⲛⲟⲥ· ⲙⲛ ⲟⲩⲭⲁⲗⲓ̈ⲛⲟⲥ ⲉⲛⲉⲕⲥⲡⲟⲧⲟⲩ· ⲧⲁⲕⲕⲧⲟⲕ ϩⲛ ⲧⲉϩⲓ̈ⲏ ⲛⲧⲁⲕⲉⲓ ϩⲣⲁⲓ̈ ⲛϩⲏⲧⲥ· ⲟⲩⲙⲁⲉⲓⲛ ⲇⲉ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲁⲓ̈· ⲟⲩⲱⲙ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲓⲣⲟⲙⲡⲉ ⲛ̅ⲛⲉⲛⲧⲁⲕϫⲟⲟⲩ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲥⲉⲉⲡⲉ ϩⲛ̅ ⲧⲙⲉϩⲥⲛⲧⲉ· ϩⲛ̅
the Lord says, the God of Israel: | have heard (the things) you have prayed to me concerning Sennacherim the King of the Assyrians. 22 This is the word that the Lord has spoken concerning him: He has despised you and mocked you, O Virgin, Daughter of Sion! And he has shaken his head at you, O Daughter of lerousalem! 23 Whom have you reviled and whom have you provoked? And against whom have you blasphemed? Or against whom have you raised your hand? And you did not lift your eyes on high to the Holy One of Israel! 24 Because by (your) messengers you have reproached the Lord, for you said, ‘With the multitude of my chariots | have gone up to the height of the mountains and to the utmost limit of Lebanon, and | cut down the height of its cedar and the beauty of the cypress, and | entered into the height of its forest region, 25 and | built a bridge and desolated the waters and every gathering of water.’ 26 Have you not heard long ago of these things that | have done from ancient days and ordained them? For now | have begun to make desolate the strong nations that are in strong places and (those who) dwell in high cities. 27 They have loosened their hands, and they have withered, (and) they have become like dry grass on housetops and like wild grass. 28 But now | know your resting place, your going out and your coming in. 29 (And) your wrath with which you have raged and your bitterness have come up to me. And | will put a muzzle on your nose and a bit on your lips; and | will turn you back on the way by which you came. 30 And this shall be the sign for you: This year eat what you have sown,

and in the second (year) what is left; then in the third (year) you will sow, reap, and plant vineyards, and eat their fruits. 31 And those that are left in the land of Juda shall take root and bear seed upward, 32 because in lerousalem shall be those who are left, and from Mount Sion those who will be saved. The zeal of the Lord will do these things. 33 Therefore thus says the Lord concerning the king of the Assyrians: He shall not come into this city nor cast an arrow against it nor bring a shield against it nor set up a rampart around it. 34 But he shall return by the (same) way that he came. This is what the Lord says: 35 ‘I will shield this city to save it, for my own sake and for the sake of my servant Dauid.” 36 And (then) the angel of the Lord set out and destroyed one hundred eighty-five thousand out of the camp of the Assyrians; when they arose in the early morning, they found all the dead bodies. 37 Then the king of the Assyrians turned back, departed and lived at Nineue. 38 And as he was doing obeisance in his house to Nasarach his patachron, his sons Adramech and Saraas smote him with daggers, but they escaped into Armenia. And his son Nachorda reigned in his stead

38 1 Now it happened at that time (that) Hezekias became sick to the point of death. And the prophet Esaias son of Amos came to him and said to him, “This is what the Lord God of Israel says: Make arrangements concerning your house, for you shall die; and you shall not live.” 2 Then Hezekias turned his face to the wall and prayed to the Lord 3 saying: “Remember, O Lord,

how | have walked before you with truth and with a true heart, and have done the thing that is pleasing in your sight.” And Hezekias wept with a great weeping. 4 Then the word of the Lord came to Esaias, saying: 5 “Go, and say to Hezekias, This is what the Lord, the God of Dauid your father says: | have heard the voice of your prayer and have seen your tears; look, | am adding fifteen years to the time of your life. 6 And | have saved you out of the hand of the king of the Assyrians and | will shield this city. 7 For this will be the sign to you from the Lord, that God will do this thing 8 Behold | will turn back the shadow of your steps on which the sun has gone down — the steps of the house of your father — | will turn back the sun ten steps.” So the sun went up the ten steps on which the shadow had gone down. 9 Prayer of Hezekias — the King of Judea, when he had become sick and had recovered from his sickness: 10 “I said at the height of my days: | shall leave (behind) the rest of my years in the gates of Hades. 11 | said, No longer shall | see the salvation of God on the earth; no longer shall | see aman 12 from my kindred. | have left behind the rest of my life. My spirit has gone out from me (and) departed like the one who having pitched a tent took it down; (my spirit) in me became like a tunic on a loom that (= tunic) was about to be cut off. 13 In that day (it was) as if | have been given over to a lion until morning; thus he broke my bones, for | was given over from day to night. 14 | will cry out like a swallow, and | will mutter like a dove; for my eyes have failed from looking to the height of the heaven, toward the

ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲉⲛⲧⲁϥⲛⲁϩⲙⲉⲧ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁϥϥⲓ ⲙⲡϣⲛ̅ϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ· ⲁⲩⲧⲁⲙⲟⲕ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲉⲧⲃⲏⲏⲧⲥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲕⲧⲟⲩⲛⲉⲥ ⲡⲁⲛⲓ̈ϥⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲉⲣⲉⲕⲥⲱⲗⲧ̅ ⲁⲕⲧⲁⲛϩⲟⲓ̈· ⲁⲕⲧⲟⲩϫⲟ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ϫⲉ ⲛⲛⲉⲥⲧⲁⲕⲟ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲁⲱⲛ̅ϩ̅· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲁⲕⲕⲧⲟ ⲛⲛⲁⲛⲟⲃⲉ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲉⲡⲁϩⲟⲩ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ̈· ⲛⲉⲧϩⲛ̅ ⲁⲙⲛⲧⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲁϩⲱⲥ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲉⲛⲧⲁⲩⲙⲟⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲁⲥⲙⲟⲩ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ· ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛⲉⲧϩⲛ̅ ⲁⲙⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛⲁϩⲉⲗⲡⲓ̈ⲍⲉ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲧⲉⲕⲙⲛ̅ⲧⲛⲁ· ⲛⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ⲛⲉⲧⲛⲁⲥⲙⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲁϩⲉ· ϫⲓ̈ⲛ ⲉⲡⲟⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ϯⲛⲁϫⲡⲟ ⲛϩⲉⲛϣⲏⲣⲉ· ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲛⲁϫⲱ ⲛⲧⲉⲕⲙⲛ̅ⲧⲛⲁ· ⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ⲙⲡⲁⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ̈· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛϯⲛⲁⲗⲟ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲓ̈ⲥⲙⲟⲩ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ϩⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲯⲁⲗⲧⲏⲣⲓ̈ⲟⲛ ⲛⲛⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲙⲡⲁⲱⲛϩ· ⲙⲡⲉⲙⲧⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲡⲏⲓ̈ ⲙⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲏⲥⲁⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ⲡⲉⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲛⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ϫⲉ ϫⲓ̈ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲛⲟⲩⲧⲁϭ ⲛⲕⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛ̅ⲅ̅ⲧⲛ̅ⲛⲟϥ ⲛ̅ⲅⲗⲁⲗⲱⲱⲕ ⲙⲙⲟϥ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲕⲛⲁⲙ̅ⲧⲟⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ϫⲉ
Lord, who rescued me and took away 15 the pain of my soul. 16 Lord, you were indeed told concerning it, and you revived my breath, and when you comforted me, you revived me! 17 For you have saved my soul so that it may not perish, and my life, and you have rejected all my sins behind me. 18 For those who are in Hades will not praise you, nor will the dead bless you, nor will those who are in Hades hope for your mercy. 19 The living will praise (you), as | also do, for from today | will produce children who will declare your mercy, 20 O Lord of my salvation. And | will not cease praising you with a harp all the days of my life, before the house of the Lord.” 21 And the prophet Esaias said to Hezekias, “Take a cake of figs, and mash it, and smear yourself with it, and you will be well.” 22 And Hezekias said, “This is the sign that | shall go up to the house of the Lord God.”

39 1 At that time Magotach the son of Laadan, the king of Babylonia, sent letters and ambassadors and presents to Hezekias, for he heard that he had been sick to the point of death and had recovered. 2 And Hezekias rejoiced over them with great joy, and he showed them the house of the nechotha and of the oil of myrrh and of the incense and of the perfume and of the silver and of the gold and all the houses of the vessels of the treasures and all the things that were in his storehouses. And there was nothing that Hezekias did not show them in his house. 3 Then the prophet Esaias came to the King Hezekias (and) said to him, “What do these men say, and from where have they come to you?” And Hezekias said, “They have come to me from a land far away, from Babylon.” 4 And Esaias said, “What have they seen in your house?” And Hezekias said, “They have seen all the things that are in my house — and there is nothing in my house that they have not seen — but also the other things in my storehouses.” 5 Then Esaias said: “Hear the word of the Lord Sabaoth: 6 Look, days


[p. 648] {#po.57.p648}
ϩⲏⲏⲧⲉ ⲟⲩⲛ̅ ϩⲉⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲏⲩ ⲛⲥⲉϫⲓ ⲛ̅ⲛ̅ⲕⲁ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲉⲧϩⲙ̅ ⲡⲉⲕⲏⲓ̈· ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲛⲧⲁⲛⲉⲕⲉⲓⲟⲧⲉ ⲥⲟⲟⲩϩⲟⲩ ⲉϩⲟⲩⲛ ϣⲁ ⲡⲟⲟⲩ ⲛϩⲟⲟⲩ· ⲛⲥⲉⲉⲛⲧⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲃⲁⲃⲩⲗⲱⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲛⲥⲉⲧⲙ̅ⲕⲁ ⲗⲁⲁⲩ· ⲁⲡⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉ ⲇⲉ ϫⲟⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϫⲓ̈ ϩⲉⲛ ⲛⲉⲕϣⲏⲣⲉ ⲉⲛⲧⲁⲕϫⲡⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲥⲉⲁⲁⲩ ⲛⲥⲓⲟⲩⲣ ϩⲙ ⲡⲏⲓ̈ ⲙⲡⲣⲣⲟ ⲛⲧⲃⲁⲃⲩⲗⲱⲛ· ⲁⲩⲱ ⲡⲉϫⲉ ⲉⲍⲉⲕⲓ̈ⲁⲥ ⲛⲏⲥⲁⲓ̈ⲁⲥ· ϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲛⲟⲩ ⲡϣⲁϫⲉ ⲛⲧⲁⲡϫⲟⲉⲓ̈ⲥ ϫⲟⲟϥ· ⲙⲁⲣⲉⲟⲩⲉⲓⲣⲏⲛⲏ ⲙ̅ⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲇⲓ̈ⲉⲁⲓⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ϣⲱⲡⲉ ϩⲛ̅ ⲛⲁϩⲟⲟⲩ·
are coming, when they will take all the things that are in your house and whatever your fathers have collected up until this day and they shall be brought to Babylon, and they shall leave nothing behind. And God has said 7 that they shall take (also) some of your children whom you have begotten and shall make them eunuchs in the house of the king of Babylon.” 8 Then Hezekias said to Esaias, “The word that the Lord has spoken is good. Let there (now) be peace and righteousness in my days.”
